Replies came from: Claude (live Anthropic API)

Code: commit 7c991909cd2567b8962004b0e12666ab833695ee, uncommitted changes: True | seed 7, calendars from 2026-10-28

HOW TO USE THIS FILE
  Read it top to bottom, or paste or upload it into any AI chat and ask questions, for example:
    - What is group 2026-10-31/g1, and does it make sense for these people?
    - Who said what to whom about it, and why did each person's agent answer the way it did?
    - What is e117's personality, and did their agent stay true to it all run?
    - Was anyone placed into something their profile rules out? Who was left out, and why?
    - Did the hub claim things about people that their profiles don't support?
  Ask the AI to cite event numbers like [#560]: every one points to the verbatim record in
  full_simulation.jsonl and full_transcript.txt in this run's folder.

WHAT THE WORDS MEAN
  hub          the Clawnly matchmaker (orchestrator), claude-opus-5-5. It plans groups and talks to each agent one to one.
  agent        one emulated person's own AI, claude-sonnet-5. It knows only that person's profile and answers for them.
               Agents never talk to each other. Each agent call is a fresh conversation: no memory.
  break room   a pool code builds per activity and time of day: everyone free, interested, within
               budget and not avoiding anything about it. The hub may only invite from it.
  keenness     how much a person likes the activity's tags (likes are weighted 1-3).
  round 1 / 2  round 1 asks everyone the plan proposed; round 2 asks only who the hub re-asks after
               the resolve step (alternates for a no, people whose time changed).
  yes/no/counter  an agent's answer; a counter proposes one change.
  locked       code confirms a group once 2+ people said yes; dropped otherwise.
  code showed the agent   what matchmaker.plan_checks found in the profile against the plan.

SECTIONS
  1. PEOPLE: personality prompts, before and after
  2. CONVERSATIONS: every message, day by day and group by group
  3. GROUPS: each group and its logic
  4. TIMELINE: every event, one line each
  5. WHAT OUR CHECKS FOUND (read last; skip it for an independent review)

====================================================================================================
1. PEOPLE: each emulated person's personality prompt (before) and what happened to them (after)
====================================================================================================

THE AGENT PROMPT IN FULL, exactly as e001's agent got it as its system prompt.
Everyone's prompt has this exact wording; only the name and the part under 'TOLD YOU' differ.
Code checked this for every person below; anyone whose prompt differs has it printed in full.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    You are the personal agent of Nikhil R.. You know Nikhil only from their profile and calendar below. You speak for Nikhil to the Clawnly matchmaker. You never talk to other people's agents.
    
    WHAT NIKHIL TOLD YOU:
    Nikhil is 37, a commuter in Lake Sawyer, with kids at home.
    Loves: golf.
    Really likes: watch sports, crafts.
    Not into: history, book club, dining.
    Avoids: late nights, early mornings, spending money.
    Budget: free or low-cost things.
    This week: Wednesday 28 Oct: busy; Thursday 29 Oct: busy; Friday 30 Oct: busy; Saturday 31 Oct: free afternoon, evening; Sunday 1 Nov: busy; Monday 2 Nov: busy; Tuesday 3 Nov: busy.
    
    Check the plan against everything above before you answer: the time against their calendar and what they avoid, the activity against their likes and dislikes, the cost against their budget. Code has listed what it found; weigh it, and look for anything it missed.
    What Nikhil avoids is a limit they set, not a preference to trade off: if the plan runs into it, say no (or counter, if the time can move), however much they'd enjoy the rest.
    - "yes" only with a strong reason from the profile: something Nikhil loves or really likes that this plan gives them. Put that reason in "reason"; a yes without one counts as a no.
    - "no" if something clearly conflicts and no small change fixes it, or if there is no strong reason. Being only up for it (a mild interest) is not a strong reason. Saying no is normal and helps the matchmaker.
    - "counter" with one concrete change that would make it a yes (for example a later start inside a day part they're free). A fixed-time event can't move, so don't counter its time.
    Reply with only this JSON object: {"answer": "yes" or "no" or "counter", "reason": "for a yes: the strong reason, from the profile", "counter": "the change, if any", "say": "one or two sentences to the matchmaker, as Nikhil's agent"}
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  THE HUB'S REASONING (the API's summary of its thinking) [#477]
    I need to map out up to ten groups and check each fixed event against people's preferences, like avoiding early mornings or late nights — things like the kraken slot ending 9:30pm or the vault event running until 10pm could conflict with people who dislike late nights.
    
    For the kraken evening, I'm sorting through candidates by interest overlap: craft beer fans, sports watchers, and dining types, while excluding anyone flagged as avoiding late nights.
    
    For the vault music event, I'm grouping together the live-music and craft-beer enthusiasts, but being careful since a crowded beer garden concert might not suit someone who avoids crowds — so I'm shifting that person to a walk group instead and finalizing roughly three people for vault and a similar small set for kraken.
    
    For game night, I'm weighing whether to include a board-games fan or let the group stand with card and board game lovers alone, then for the dog-focused evening I'm settling on four dog lovers, scheduling earlier to respect one person's preference against late nights.
    
    With alternates e112 and e076 noted for the game night group, I'm now sorting the lumber-house/big-block crowd: pairing e158 and e085 for big-block since both like craft beer and avoid loud places, sending e195 to the vault, then grouping e062 and e033 for a 5-7pm lumber-house slot since both favor craft beer, with e051 as an alternate.
    
    For the ten-trails evening walk, I'm weighing hikers e105 and e171 against possible additions like e042 (tagged running, which fits the walk theme) or e174, leaning toward e105, e171, and e042 as the core group with e174 and e195 as alternates since e042 has fewer existing plans.
    
    Shifting to afternoon activities, I'm mapping out participant lists for the geyser hike, Franklin trip, gorge outing, potluck, Sawyer paddle, and museum visit, noting which attendees overlap across multiple options like e053, e154, e023, e016, e086, e041, e102, and e124. For the morning slot, I'm checking who's free for the Franklin morning activity, including e020, e154, e106, e086, e136, and e064.
    
    I'm now capping things at 10 groups total, and I've already committed to seven: vault, kraken, sawyer-dogs, game-night, big-block, lumber-house evening, and ten-trails walk — that's a lot weighted toward evening. I'm weighing whether to cut lumber-house evening since it's only two people, or drop big-block instead, favoring whichever groups cover more participants overall.
    
    I'm merging big-block and lumber's members into one lumber-house evening group of four people, which lets me drop big-block entirely and simplifies the evening slate. That leaves me with six evening groups covering roughly 20 people, and only four slots left for morning/afternoon, so I'm debating whether game night or kraken should also get cut to make room.
    
    For the day groups, I'm building a potluck afternoon with five people focused on cooking and parents with kids, running from noon to 2:30pm. I'm also sketching a Franklin afternoon hiking group pulling from people interested in hiking, history, and photography, still narrowing down the exact combination.
    
    Then I'm adding a trail cleanup morning from 9-12 with five volunteers who favor hiking and morning activities, and a Gorge morning session anchored by three photographers around Morgan Creek and Ten Trails, with two hikers as alternates — bringing the count to ten groups total.
    
    Looking at remaining unplaced people, I consider using the paddle afternoon kayaking slot versus smaller fixed events like the kraken outing or game night. I think about folding the kraken group by reassigning its members into the lumber-house evening and game night groups instead, freeing up the paddle afternoon slot for others.
    
    Game night settles at four people, and paddle afternoon fills with kayakers plus one boater, keeping cost low with a couple alternates in reserve. For Franklin afternoon, I pick five people avoiding early mornings and crowds, including the hiker.
    
    For the gorge morning session timed 9-11:30am, I'm checking the late-night and early-morning constraints and settling on who fits, with an alternate backup. Trail cleanup alternates need care since some candidates already belong to other groups, so I'm sorting out who's free to serve as backup without double-booking anyone.
    
    Potluck just needs one alternate. For the vault group, I'm verifying everyone avoids late nights and checking constraints like crowd aversion and spending caution against the venue's likely conditions, accepting minor mismatches as acceptable. For the Sawyer-dogs slot (5:30-6:30pm), I'm confirming the free-cost constraint is satisfied and deciding whether to leave the alternate slot empty since the only candidate is already used elsewhere.
    
    For the evening lumber slot (5-7pm), I'm working through alternates since several candidates are already assigned, narrowing down to one viable option. Game night gets two alternates, and the ten-trails walk (5:30-7pm) also needs its alternate resolved by substituting out used candidates. Paddle is fixed at 1-3:30pm. Now I'm ready to write concise proposals for all ten event groups: trail-cleanup, gorge morning, Franklin afternoon, potluck, paddle, ten-trails walk, Sawyer-dogs, lumber, game night, and vault.
